# Multi-Level Work Breakdown Structure (WBS)

> **Companion design document**. This document accompanies
> [RFC-039 Compiler Architecture Refactoring](../../rfc/accepted/039-compiler-architecture.md). It
> expands the RFC’s phase sequence into **independently committable and independently verifiable**
> three-level tasks.
>
> The phase order and acceptance gates are governed solely by the main text of RFC-039. This
> document is responsible only for breaking the work down into executable granularity and
> registering conflicts that remain unadjudicated.

## Positioning and Scope

| Level       | Count       | Meaning                                                                                                |
| ----------- | ----------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| **Level 1** | 11 (P0–P10) | Phases, corresponding one-to-one with the phase sequence in RFC-039                                    |
| **Level 2** | 73          | Task groups: work groups broken down by phase, each of which can be staffed and archived independently |
| **Level 3** | 262         | Independently committable and independently verifiable work units                                      |

**Numbering gaps (not reused)**: P4 has no `4.7`/`4.8`, and P8 has no `8.1`; after the LALRPOP route
was withdrawn, `8.4` / `8.5` / `8.6` were also left unused (2026-11-25). All three have had no
corresponding entries since the WBS was established—the document’s entire history has zero matches
for the three headings `**4.7` / `**4.8` / `**8.1`
(`git log -S'**4.7' -- docs/src/dev/architecture/09-execution-wbs.md` can be used for verification),
and there are no repository-wide references either. `8.1` corresponds precisely to phase 1 of `05`
(test revival and completion): that phase has been split into P1 (`1.1`–`1.3`) and `8.8`, so P8’s
numbering is aligned with its phase number (phase 0 → `8.0`, phase 2 → `8.2`, and so on).

**Covered by this document**: task breakdown, dependencies, parallelization groups, and binding
acceptance criteria.

**Not covered by this document**: the design rationale for tasks (see `02`–`08`) or the rationale
for phase ordering (see RFC-039).

**Registry convention (effective 2026-10-12)**: newly registered Level 2/Level 3 rows **must contain
two fields**—“**Owning Phase**” (the P to which the task truly belongs, which may differ from the
containing section) and “**Blocks the Mainline**”. `Pending scheduling` is no longer the default
status; an unmarked entry is considered **not schedulable**. Numbers must be **unique across the
entire document** (tasks on external tracks use a track number, such as `029g.x`, and must not
occupy `N.x`).

**Closure registry (effective 2026-11-24)**: checked rows contain only four items—the task ID,
status, **implementation commit short hash** (7 characters), and issue number, when present—written
on one line: `- [x] **3.24.1 Remaining pattern-matching work** ✅ 4d608df3 (issue #405)`. **Do not
write the fix, the criterion chain, gate values, file line numbers, test names, or review
conclusions**: those belong in the commit message (`git show <short-hash>`). This table is a
dashboard, not a process chronicle. **Do not write adjudication labels or restate conclusions**:
phrases such as “User adjudication A/B/C”, “Option A”, or “The final implementation is…” must not
appear in this table—the dashboard answers only “Is it complete?”, while adjudications and results
are recorded in commit messages and the RFC-039 decision registry.

**Short hashes are authoritative only after the push**: a closure row contains the short hash on
`origin/dev` after the push has completed. The order is **push the implementation commit first, then
write the closure row** (for hash-selection rules, see [commit-convention](../commit-convention.md),
“Commit hashes are taken after push”). The closure commit itself carries no hash—self-reference is
impossible.

**Count columns contain numbers only**: the Level 1/2/3 counts under “Positioning and Scope” contain
only the current values; change history such as `+1 = …` does not belong in the table.

## Current State: Gaps Found During Breakdown

While consolidating the “Implementation Points” from eight documents into this table, **five hard
gaps in the documentation system itself** were found. All have now been resolved:

| #   | Gap                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Resolution                                                                                                                                                |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| G1  | **P5 had no implementation steps whatsoever**. RFC-039 defined P5 (checker splitting) and its acceptance criterion (C1 zero-diff), but none of the seven documents described how to perform the split; the directory tree in `01-routing.md` contained one mention of `annotations.rs`, the only such reference in the entire batch                                                                                                              | P5’s steps are supplied by this document (see below)                                                                                                      |
| G2  | **The three `include!` refactoring sites had ambiguous ownership and no implementation**. RFC-039 assigned them to P4; `02` assigned them to itself; S7 in `06` also listed them. However, the change list in `02` (6 additions + 20 modifications) did **not** include `semantic_tokens.rs`                                                                                                                                                     | Implementation steps are assigned to §P5 5.1 in this document (after P4 is complete); references in RFC-039 and `06` have been synchronized to point here |
| G3  | **Three coexisting inventories of CI scripts**. RFC-039 lists 4 `scripts/ci/*.py` scripts; `01` lists 4 scripts + 1 `cargo test`; `07` lists another 4 checks under entirely different names; `04` also previously listed `check-synth-boundary.py` (that script was cancelled by the user on 2026-11-24). After deduplication, there are **at least 9 new scripts and no unified inventory**; `check-obligations.py` is owned by both P4 and P9 | See §P9 in this document for the unified inventory (10 active scripts, each owned by one phase); all other documents refer to this table                  |
| G4  | **Conflict over the fate of `precedence_inline.rs`**. `06` counted it among the “1,005 lines to be restored”, while B6 in the same document said it “must be deleted together with” the `Precedence` enum; `05` advocated deletion                                                                                                                                                                                                               | **Restore in P1, retain in P8** (decision registry C1; Pratt is no longer to be deleted)                                                                  |
| G5  | **Conflict in the wording of the L2 goal**: RFC-039 and the index in this directory once said “grammar-driven syntax”                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | This has converged to **a declarative precedence table + an unambiguous language surface** ([05](05-frontend-paradigm.md) §Syntax), see P8 8.3            |

## Target Design: Phase Sequence

```
P0 仓库维护机制与代码放置规程 08 ← 新增，先立规矩
P1 复活孤儿测试并修缺陷 06 §S1
P2 建立等价性判据基线 07 全部
P3 修正确性漏洞（最小方案） 02 §S3-漏洞半部
P4 阶段契约与统一 Driver 02
P5 checker 文件内拆分 02 + 本文补齐
P6 类型表示单一化 03
P7 中间表示 SSA 化 04 } 可并行
P8 前端范式变更 05 }
P9 防反弹门禁（含统一脚本清单） 01 §防反弹 + 08 §可机检规则
P10 其余清理与状态修正 06 §S2/S3/S4/S5/S6
```

**Rationale for adding P0**: the three prohibitions (do not invent; do not fill endlessly; do not
patch when a refactoring is required) constrain every action in P1–P10. The rules must be
established before work begins; otherwise, while P1 restores tests, it will already be adding `mod`
declarations to `tests/mod.rs`—precisely the cause of the G4 conflict.

### P3 Correctness Vulnerabilities (Minimal Solution)

Source: [02](02-stage-contract.md) §Implementation Points S3, vulnerability-fix half ＋ **2026-10-07
silent-drop-point audit (3.4)**

> **Semantic-correction routing (D59, adjudication of 2026-10-11)**: if this track or any subsequent
> phase discovers a defect for which “the old behavior does not count as success”—**A silent failure
> / B silent mismatch / C undefined behavior and uncontrolled resources** (crashes, stack
> exhaustion, silently incorrect answers)—it **may be fixed alongside the refactoring**, but all
> four requirements are mandatory: ① red before green (the red criterion must exist first and must
> actually be red); ② do not change the externally observable behavior of programs that “previously
> compiled successfully and ran correctly” (they must not become failures, and their existing
> diagnostic codes/text/exit codes must not be removed or modified); ③ register it in the same
> commit (this row must identify the error type + the phase revision note + the PR required block
> line “Error Type”); ④ regenerate the baseline in the same commit + manually review the diff. **D
> user-visible semantics / E external-contract tables / F RFC-level design changes still require
> prior adjudication. See [07 §Semantic-Correction Routing Criteria](07-equivalence-oracle.md) for
> the expanded criteria and A–F allowlist; **“default super-refactoring + after-the-fact
> registration” is not accepted** (rationale: RFC-039 D59—without a red criterion, fixes cannot be
> distinguished from regressions, and this conflicts with “no C5′ exists” and “no relaxation
> whatsoever”).

> **Batch (adjudication of 2026-10-14)**: the four small items 3.13 / 3.14 / 3.15 / 3.16 are
> **highly related** (method binding, resolution, and the inference entry point all belong to one
> path; 3.16 is another carrier of the same “named arguments” criterion—the stdlib native export),
> and are **merged into one team**—the team scope is relaxed from “one Level 2 task” to “a batch of
> related small items”; the batch composition, rationale, and deviations must be registered when
> that team closes.

| 3.22 Decomposition semantics for function-type annotations (issue [#501](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/501); source = batch-review F4 investigation from 4.10 round 2): the correctly (the parser has been experimentally ruled out). **Owning phase: P3; blocks the mainline: no**                                   | 3.22.1 See below            | —                                                                                          | **Terminal criterion**: see 3.22.1                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
|                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | 3.22.1 Unify annotation decomposition for “returning a function type”: for `() -> ((x: Int) -> Int)`, the value-parameter group = `()`, the return = `fn(int64)->int64`, and the inner `Fn` **must not** be absorbed into the parameter group; align the expected form on the lambda-body path with it (currently there are two sets). The criterion covers four forms: lambda body / function-name RHS / no inner parentheses / scalar-return control (✅ 7880ee17, f032582b; issues [#501](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/501), [#514](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/514), and [#515](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/515)) **Acceptance**: the vulnerability criterion turns green, and **deliberately removing the fix must
turn it red again**. **Risk**: the newly added E4018 on the multi-file path is a breaking change.

**3.4 acceptance**: 3.4.1 / 3.4.2 must have red criteria first (changing code first is prohibited);
3.4.3 requires manual review; 3.4.4–3.4.8 use C2/C3 and the 9.2 gate as the criterion. **Risk**: the
new diagnostic in 3.4.1 is a behavior change, so the corpus differential baseline and CHANGELOG must
be synchronized.

> **Emergency stopgap path**: P3 does not need to wait for all of P2. The only hard prerequisites
> for 3.1/3.2 are **2.3.2 (multi-file corpus tier) + 2.4.1/2.4.3 (two red criteria)**;
> 2.1/2.2/2.3.1/2.3.3 (IR verifier, snapshots, single-file differential testing, and performance
> baseline) may proceed in parallel with P3. Stopping the correctness vulnerability (`Sorted(3)`
> silently passing) must not be blocked by snapshot-infrastructure construction. 3.3
> (panic-to-diagnostic conversion) has no prerequisites and can land at any time.

### External Track RFC-029g: Remove `pub` and Automatic Binding (External Track / #399 — Ordering Constraint D56)

Source: [RFC-029g](../../rfc/accepted/029g-remove-pub-and-auto-bind.md) (accepted on 2026-10-02;
implementation not started), [#399](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/399). **This track
is not a design item of the refactoring itself; it is a specification-compliance deletion forced by
an ordering constraint**, and is distinct from P10 S5’s “reduce the visibility of `pub` items”
(Rust’s `pub(crate)` narrowing).

**Why it must wholly precede P4**: 029g’s deletion surface overlaps the rewriting surfaces of all
four refactoring phases. Reversing the order means redoing the same deletions and baseline updates
on the new grammar / new checker layout / new SSA IR.

| 029g deletion surface                                               | Files                                                                                                              | Rewritten by which phase                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `KwPub` and grammar branches                                        | `lexer/{tokens,state,mod}.rs`; `parser/parser_state.rs`, `statements/{declarations,imports,functions,bindings}.rs` | **P8** (8.2 lexer convergence / 8.3 Pratt precedence convergence / 8.7 split `parse_assign_after_target`)                                                                                                                                                                     |
| AST `is_pub` ×2 (`Assign` / `TypeDefinition`)                       | `parser/ast.rs`                                                                                                    | **P6 6.5 dead AST variants** + P8 + D1 directory rename (`parser/ast.rs` → top-level `src/ast/`)                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `auto_bind_to_type` and the `pub` branch of `collect_exports`       | `typecheck/checker.rs`, `environment.rs`                                                                           | **P5** (`include!` → true `mod` and checker splitting are **pure moves**, and the `pub` branch will be moved verbatim into the new files)                                                                                                                                     |
| Dead-code exemptions `exempt_pub` / `is_exported` and role dispatch | `typecheck/passes/dead_code.rs`, `module/orchestrator.rs` (inside the `check_project` body)                        | **P4 4.2.2** (slimming down `check_project`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| The `Public` modifier of `semantic_tokens`                          | `typecheck/checker/semantic_tokens.rs`                                                                             | **P4 4.2.6** + P5 + 4.6 (D55)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `ir_gen`’s `is_pub`-ignoring binding                                | `middle/core/ir_gen.rs`                                                                                            | **P7** (SSA transformation of `ir_gen.rs`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| 31 occurrences of `pub` in `src/std/`                               | `std/list.yx` / `json.yx` / `option.yx` / `result.yx`                                                              | No phase directly changes them, but **P6’s hard acceptance criterion requires zero differences in `src/std/` (`git diff --stat src/std/` is empty; direct-driver tests under `tests/` are exempt as described in P6’s hard acceptance section)**—they can only land before P6 |

**Ancillary benefit**: 029g removes the dead `env.exports` table (filled by the producer, with zero
repository-wide consumers) and the `is_exported`/`is_visible` accessors ⇒ P4’s 4.3 obligation ledger
loses one more “produced but never consumed” field.

- [x] **029g.1 Lexer and parser branches**
- [x] **029g.2 AST `is_pub` ×2**
- [x] **029g.3 Checker side**
- [x] **029g.4 Dead-code exemption convergence**
- [x] **029g.5 Peripheral echo**
- [x] **029g.6 Stdlib and diagnostic text**
- [x] **029g.7 Language-reference convergence**

| Level 2                    | Level 3                                                                                                                      | Files                                                                                                                | Acceptance                                                                                                                                                                             |
| -------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 029g.1 Lexer and parser    | 029g.1.1 Delete the `KwPub` variant and the `keyword_from_str` entry                                                         | `lexer/tokens.rs`, `state.rs`, `mod.rs`                                                                              | Keyword table 18→17 (`Kw*` 16→15, including the count comment in `tokens.rs`)                                                                                                          |
|                            | 029g.1.2 Delete `pub` detection and `pre_detected_pub` at declaration positions                                              | `parser/statements/declarations.rs`, `functions.rs`, `bindings.rs`                                                   | `pub f = ...` reports a parse error (**red criterion first**)                                                                                                                          |
|                            | 029g.1.3 Delete `pub` skipping for entries inside `use` braces and leading `pub` before statements                           | `parser/statements/imports.rs`, `parser_state.rs`                                                                    | `pub use m` reports a parse error (**red criterion first**)                                                                                                                            |
| 029g.2 AST                 | 029g.2.1 Delete `is_pub` from `Assign`/`TypeDefinition` + synchronize construction sites                                     | `parser/ast.rs` and each construction site                                                                           | No `pub` remnants in the compiler (`grep -rn is_pub src/` is empty)                                                                                                                    |
| 029g.3 Checker             | 029g.3.1 Delete `auto_bind_to_type` (`pub` was its only reason for existing)                                                 | `checker.rs`, `environment.rs`, call site `checker.rs`                                                               | Method form retains only explicit composition (RFC-004), with zero stdlib/corpus dependencies                                                                                          |
|                            | 029g.3.2 Remove the `pub` branch from `collect_exports` + delete the dead `env.exports` table and `is_exported`/`is_visible` | `checker.rs`, `environment.rs`                                                                                       | The sole authoritative export surface = module registry `ModuleInfo.exports`; the 9.2 gate loses one field                                                                             |
| 029g.4 Dead-code exemption | 029g.4.1 Delete `exempt_pub` / `is_exported` and the exemption branch                                                        | `dead_code.rs`                                                                                                       | Unreferenced top-level bindings in Lib/Script report W1001 (**red criterion first**)                                                                                                   |
|                            | 029g.4.2 Synchronize `orchestrator` role dispatch                                                                            | `orchestrator.rs`                                                                                                    | Single-file / multi-file diagnostic sets are normalized (C2), and W codes do not leak into consumers                                                                                   |
| 029g.5 Peripheral echo     | 029g.5.1 The formatter’s `pub` prefix                                                                                        | `formatter/handlers/stmt.rs`                                                                                         | format is idempotent (existing criterion)                                                                                                                                              |
|                            | 029g.5.2 The `Public` modifier of `semantic_tokens`                                                                          | `typecheck/checker/semantic_tokens.rs`                                                                               | Semantic highlighting no longer emits Public                                                                                                                                           |
| 029g.6 Stdlib and text     | 029g.6.1 Delete 31 `pub` prefixes in `src/std`                                                                               | `std/{list,json,option,result}.yx`                                                                                   | Corpus and stdlib self-checks are green; **must be completed before P6**                                                                                                               |
|                            | 029g.6.2 Diagnostic text                                                                                                     | 4 W1xxx help messages                                                                                                | No longer claim that “pub is an external interface”; aligned with Bin-role behavior in `dead_code.rs`                                                                                  |
| 029g.7 Documentation       | 029g.7.1 Language reference and guides                                                                                       | `reference/language-spec/{modules,syntax,language-overview}.md`, `warning-code/warning-codes.md`, `guide/modules.md` | The specification no longer contains statements contrary to the implementation, such as “pub can still be written”; “pub never triggers W1001” in `warning-codes.md` is also corrected |

**Acceptance (behavior changes; red criterion first)**:

1. **Red before green**: each of the two behavior changes must first have a red criterion before
   code is changed—① `pub` changes from “silently accepted” to a parse error; ② unreferenced
   top-level bindings in Lib/Script roles newly report W1001 (previously `pub` items were absolutely
   exempt).
2. **The test count must not decrease**: when deleting the `exempt_pub` case and `is_pub` fixture in
   `typecheck/passes/tests/dead_code.rs`, they must be replaced by equivalent new cases.
3. **Baseline in the same commit**: diagnostic-set changes (a new parse error + a new W1001) ⇒ the
   corpus differential baseline (`tests/baselines/corpus-parity.jsonl`) and CHANGELOG are updated in
   the same commit; the IR snapshot is expected to have zero differences, and any non-zero
   difference requires an explanation.
4. **Independent revert unit**: the entire batch occupies one commit group and must not be mixed
   with other phases (DoD 6).
5. `cargo test` / `clippy -D warnings` / `python scripts/rfc/check_tracking.py` are all green.

**Ordering**: overlaps with 3.4 in files (`checker.rs`, `environment.rs`) ⇒ the two must be serial
and cannot run in parallel; **the whole track must precede P4** (RFC-039 D56). The 029g track does
not depend on 3.4; the implementer may choose which to start based on the context at hand.

> **Commit correspondence (2026-10-08)**: the incorporation of this section (the P3.5 chapter +
> main-chain/constraint row) was merged into `97071c81b` alongside the RFC-039 D56 references while
> cleaning the workspace in the parallel stream. That commit’s message records “3.4 supplementary
> registration + D57/D58 adjudications recorded” and does not mention this track. History is not
> rewritten; following the existing convention (the `d5b18390` precedent, see the P2 landing note),
> it is recorded in place as evidence for subsequent bisection.

|                                                  | 4.4.4 The shared-solver observation ledger `ConsumerStats`/`CONSUMER_STATS`: every production-path point writes, while the production side has zero readers (the only read site is `proof/smt/tests/shared.rs`). **Decision of 2026-10-24 ① retain it**—write “the sole consumer = tests” as a doc contract (preserving the observable-criterion carrier required by D58); decision ② “delete it” is not adopted (that would require simultaneously rewriting 4.4.2’s acceptance criterion; the trade-off rationale is in the 4.4 closure item “sixteen”). **Terminal state**: the three contract statements are written into the module header of `src/frontend/core/typecheck/proof/smt/backend.rs` and in relevant item documentation comments—① the write surface = every acquisition through the shared interface records one atomic increment; ② the read surface is **only** `proof/smt/tests/shared.rs` (zero calls from production code); ③ `SolverConsumer::ALL` is a **test-only constant**, and production code must not depend on it; **zero behavior change** (only comments changed, and 4.4.2’s acceptance numbers are unchanged; issue [#475](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/475)) |                                                  | 4.9.9 Calling a cross-unit single-definition method with **named arguments** loses the formal-name table (registered 2026-11-25, discovered during the 7c closure period): calling a cross-unit (registry-injected) method with named arguments reports `E1014 Function '…' has no parameter named '…' (available: )`—the name table is empty; positional calls to the same method work normally, as do named calls within a single file. Minimal reproduction: `lib.yx` defines `Point.scale_by: (self: &Point, k: Float) -> Float`, and `main.yx` contains `p.scale_by(k = 2.0)` ⇒ E1014, while `p.scale_by(2.0)` ⇒ 6.0. **D59 classification: class C** (old behavior was not success: named arguments are silently dropped, followed by runtime failure or a silently wrong value). **Scope (final form after two rounds of narrowing on 2026-11-26—only three items are added; everything else is deletion and registration)**: ① `AstToIrGenerator::method_param_info` (`src/middle/lower/query.rs`)—obtain declaration-time formal names from the unique cross-unit carrier `ModuleInfo::method_overloads` using the definition-unit qualified name `{module}.{Type}.{method}[#N]` (the candidate ↔ mixed-IR name ↔ formal-name three vectors are in the same order as on the typecheck injection side); the call site (`src/middle/lower/expr/call.rs`) uses the qualified name from `method_dispatch_name` to look up the name table, rather than two bare-name segments. These two locations fix only **bare-name imports** (`local == target`) for cross-unit methods: the candidate-injection surface retains the pre-4.9.9 narrowing rule (`local != target` is skipped), so named arguments for alias imports are outside the fix scope (see “Known Gaps”). ② `ModuleRegistry::all_method_bindings` (`src/frontend/module/registry.rs`) merges in **ascending module-key order**—**class C (independent of the original bug): eliminate the “same input, multiple results” behavior caused by HashMap iteration order**, not a fix of the original bug; criterion = the same-file unit test `test_all_method_bindings_merge_is_deterministic_across_hash_seeds` (32 registries with alternating insertion orders; only one single binding is allowed for the same method key; removing the ascending-order merge produces two results). **Criteria (red before green)**: 2 driver-level tests (`src/driver/tests/named_args_cross_unit_method.rs`) (bare import with one candidate 6.0 / two candidates selected by name 6.0 and 30, both with value assertions; file header marked as a pathology gate); unit test (`src/middle/lower/query.rs`) (`method_param_info` empty-name-table guard ⇒ None, positive control returns a non-empty table unchanged); unit test (`src/frontend/module/registry.rs`) (the deterministic criterion above). Project-level fixture `tests/yaoxiang-multifile/named-args-cross-unit-method` (the `alias-overload` fixture is deleted along with the alias-surface retirement); the corpus differential baseline `tests/baselines/corpus-parity.jsonl` now has 404 lines: **relative to direct parent commit `ac810e97`, 405→404** (the intermediate state contained `alias-overload`); **relative to the pre-4.9.9 baseline `08ccacc5`, 403→404** (+1 basic fixture `named-args-cross-unit-method`; `alias-overload` never entered the final history). The line-count change is an inevitable result of fixtures entering and leaving (after `corpus_probe` regeneration, it matches the checked-in baseline byte for byte), **not lost coverage**. **Known Gaps (each named individually; old behavior = silently ignored or silently dropped; not fixed; D60② should report but does not, class A)**: closure-value binding / block-body function declaration / named nested function declaration / built-in container constructors other than `len` named arguments / namespace arms / ordinary static-function arms / **alias-import named arguments** (candidate injection recognizes only `local == target` ⇒ alias-import candidates are not injected, the IR has no resolution, and named arguments are silently dropped; the minimal form `use a.{Temp as X}` + `t.bump(k = 2.0)` produces runtime E6007 both before 4.9.9 and after narrowing, while the positional form `t.bump(2.0)` works normally; an intermediate implementation before narrowing made it work by injecting alias candidates, and that mechanism was rolled back in this round) / whole-module import / RFC-004 binding arms. There are also two residual identity-surface issues (not fixed; migration surfaces are listed in 4.9.10): a same-named type owned by the current unit + an alias import (two same-named definition-side identities coexist, and the import surface cannot register the local declaration); function-body-level / cross-scope imports are merged at unit-global scope (a shared import table)—this shape itself does not work (an existing defect: check passes, run reports E6006), was not introduced by this batch, and is not fixed. **Deletions in this round (relative to the first six rounds of 4.9.9)**: candidate-provider priority, candidate-signature comparison, owner/compatibility inference, the shared provider table `frontend::module::import_face`, the IR-side “if the name table is unavailable ⇒ E1014” fallback, and the **E5006 ambiguity prohibition at import-name resolution points**—the latter rejected mixed/alias-only forms that had worked on the parent commit (class D), and the 2026-11-26 adjudication withdrew it; this round introduces no new ambiguity determination. **Visible changes**: ① cross-unit method named arguments (**bare-name imports**, including two-candidate overloads) change from an empty-table E1014 to slotting by name (measured 6.0 / 30); ② all other forms return to pre-4.9.9 behavior (named arguments are silently ignored or dropped, see “Known Gaps”); ③ a single method binding for the same input no longer varies with HashMap iteration order (class C). **Status**: completed on 2026-11-26 (including the narrowing round and review pass). | 4.9.7         | Criteria are in `src/driver/tests/named_args_cross_unit_method.rs`, `src/middle/lower/tests/query.rs`, and `src/frontend/module/registry.rs`; the root fix is in 4.9.10                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
|                                                  | 4.9.10 Undefined cross-unit method identity (registered 2026-11-25; the structural issue confirmed during 4.9.9 implementation): the method-candidate key `{Type}.{method}` does not include module qualification, so for same-named types across modules, three consumers—candidate injection (`driver::arms::visible_method_overloads`), typecheck formal-name/candidate matching, and IR dispatch (`method_dispatch_name`)—each guess the provider by name, and changing any one of them moves the “silent wrong value” to another location. **Root solution**: upgrade type identity to a **module-qualified identity** (`{module}.{Type}`), and change the method-candidate key to `{module}.{Type}.{method}`; one resolver produces it and all three consumers only read it. This requires changing the shape of the `overload_resolutions` key and mixed-IR names; D59 **class D** (requires adjudication). **Disposition in this round (the scope after two rounds of narrowing in 4.9.9)**: 4.9.9 **introduces no new rejection**—the class-D rejection lists from the first six rounds (all named arguments ⇒ E1014 / conflicting positional-argument forms ⇒ E1107 / E5006 ambiguity prohibition at import-name resolution points) are deleted and void; the import forms and positional arguments of mixed / alias-only / conflicting forms that worked on the parent commit are restored to a working state; **named arguments for alias imports are still silently dropped** (`use a.{Temp as X}` + `t.bump(k = 2.0)` ⇒ runtime E6007), not fixed, see “Known Gaps” in 4.9.9. **Residual surfaces (to be closed during migration)**: ① candidate keys / method keys still do not include module qualification; ② a same-named type owned by the current unit + an alias import (two same-named definition-side identities coexist); ③ function-body-level / cross-scope imports are merged at unit-global scope (a shared import table)—this shape itself does not work (an existing defect: check passes, run reports E6006); ④ named arguments for closure-value binding / block-body function declaration / named nested function declaration / built-in container constructors other than `len` / namespace arms / ordinary static-function arms / alias-import named arguments / whole-module import / RFC-004 binding arms are still silently ignored or dropped (D60② class A). **Status**: pending scheduling (class D, requires adjudication). ### P5 In-File Checker Splitting — Supplemented by This Document (Original Gap G1)

Source: RFC definition + the directory tree in `01-routing.md` + hard constraint (the `pub(crate)`
path of `collect_used_in_type` must not change)

- [x] **5.1 `include!` → true `mod`** (2 Level 3 tasks)
- [x] **5.2 Extract `refinement`** (2 Level 3 tasks)
- [x] **5.3 Extract `annotations`** (2 Level 3 tasks)

| Level 2                     | Level 3                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Prerequisites | Acceptance                                                                                                                                        |
| --------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 5.1 `include!` → true `mod` | 5.1.1 Replace the `include!` at the end of `checker.rs` with a true `mod` declaration (do not hard-code line anchors; it was measured at the end of the file at the time)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | P4 complete   | C1 zero-diff; `semantic_tokens.rs` gains module identity                                                                                          |
|                             | 5.1.2 Add `use super::*` or individual imports (`include!` implicitly inherited the surrounding scope)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | 5.1.1         | `cargo build` is green                                                                                                                            |
| 5.2 Extract `refinement`    | 5.2.1 Move out the refinement blocks (three non-contiguous intervals from the original `checker.rs`; now a single `checker/refinement.rs` file containing `ReturnRefinement` / `RefinedWalkCtx`), leaving the retained surface and six cross-boundary shared items in place                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | 5.1.1         | C1 zero-diff                                                                                                                                      |
|                             | 5.2.2 Consolidate the refinement entry point as “`&self` + return diagnostics,” **without detaching it from impl**: `collect_refined_binding_checks` (now `checker/refinement.rs`, `pub(super)`) no longer implicitly writes to the error channel through `&mut self`; the diagnostic set is returned upward, and the sole write site is the sole caller `check_module_impl` (`checker.rs`, symbol-located). **Rationale for remaining in impl**: the only field used by this family is `self.env` (a purely technical detachment is feasible), but moving the entry point and traversal entirely to free functions would also move these call sites: `resolve_type_annotation` has 8 production sites (6 annotation paths + `refined_walk_stmt` + `collect_refined_var_names_in_stmt`), `collect_param_refinements` has 2 production sites + 1 test site, and it would create a reverse dependency from the “retained surface (signature collection)” to refinement—the opposite of the coupling this step is intended to eliminate | 5.2.1         | —                                                                                                                                                 |
| 5.3 Extract `annotations`   | 5.3.1 Move out the 10-member annotation-validation family (now a single `checker/annotations.rs` file; `check_annotation_type_names` is the `pub(super)` entry point, the other 9 members are private to the family, and `is_predicate_head` is a free function within the family)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | 5.2.2         | C1 zero-diff                                                                                                                                      |
|                             | 5.3.2 **Keep `collect_used_in_type` (`pub(crate)`) as `pub(crate)` under the `checker` module path**: it is a **shared-surface** function (consumed by both `collect_function_signature` and `inference/statements.rs:16`), and 5.3.1 was **measured not to migrate it** ⇒ the “zero caller changes” criterion **was already satisfied**, so **do not add** a new `pub(crate) use annotations::*;` re-export. **Constraint retained**: if it is migrated with the family in the future (into annotations or another later batch), `checker.rs` must add a `pub(crate) use annotations::*;` re-export so that the import remains unchanged                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | 5.3.1         | **“Zero caller changes” is the hard acceptance criterion for this step: `git diff src/frontend/core/typecheck/inference/statements.rs` is empty** |

**Hard constraint**: `collect_used_in_type` is used across modules by `inference/statements.rs:16`.
**If** the function is migrated with the annotation family in the future, `checker.rs` **must then**
add a `pub(crate) use annotations::*;` re-export so that the import remains unchanged; measurement
showed that 5.3 did **not** migrate it, so this batch did not add a re-export (the criterion was
already satisfied).

**Only split blocks that are proven to be large.** Do not split modules that are only 200–400 lines
long—this adds navigation cost, reduces cohesion, and provides no decoupling benefit. The remaining
submodules (signatures / type_defs / imports) will be split after the files grow back to 1,500
lines.

**6.9 Model and Invariants (First-Principles Rewrite, 2026-11-13)**

> **Model**: D62’s end state is a **unified item world**—type positions and value positions share
> one set of compile-time items. One strength declaration produces one quantifier reading
> `q ∈ {0,1,ω}`; `q` is the **sole input** to slot allocation, and the call-site actual-argument
> view and implementation-header binding view are two projections of the same fact. Equality is a
> **pure decision** (the two sides are identical after normalization; no type variables are bound);
> evidence is the **sole entry point** for q=0 slots (four sources: literals/value
> environment/single-value pinning/Γ equalities); erasure is the **runtime consequence** of q=0
> (holding for closed terms and open terms/recursion); Σ’s constructor sites **reuse the same
> evidence interface** rather than establishing another one. **The completion criterion = the
> invariants hold, not merely that subphase names have been completed.**

| ------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ----- |

| I2 Pure equality          | defeq does not bind variables; different Refined predicates ⇒ Unproven; a decision point unifies first, rolls back all bindings on failure, and then performs defeq; one budget per decision | 6.9.3a/b/c kernel established; **6.9.3d connected production decision points** (`solver.unify`’s unify→rollback→defeq; rollback and budget protocol documented in the 6.9.3d implementation round §II)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | B     |

> **6.9 closure criterion**: I1–I5 and I7 all hold, I6 is closed according to the design-review
> conclusion for 6.9.5 (does not block P7), and I8 has evidence for every slice; residual items
> #537/#539/#540/#541/#542/#552 all have a disposition (fix/explicit exclusion/separate case); #542
> landed on 2026-11-19. **Do not substitute “all subphases checked off” for the invariants
> holding.**

> **Partial order (not a serial chain)**: A facts → {A.C3, B.d, C.2, D design}; B kernel → {B.d,
> C.2, D implementation}; C.1 is independent and complete; F4 adjudication → A.C3; decision-point
> protocol → B.d; design review → D implementation. A.C3 and B.d may run in parallel but require
> file-surface coordination (C3 leans typecheck→mono, while d leans types/solver→sema); C.2 may run
> in parallel with B.d after 6.9.2-B/6.9.3a (it does not depend on defeq); D’s implementation waits
> for confirmation of the B design package and depends on A (q=0 erasure) and C.1 (dispatch), and
> may depend on 6.9.3’s typed exit. **P7’s gate = the A/B/C exits; D does not block.**

> **A.C3’s prerequisite: F4 adjudication (A was adjudicated on 2026-11-16; measurement)**: the old
> wording “`CheckOnly` evidence does not enter requests” is obsolete—`ConstEvidence`/`CheckOnly`
> have been deleted; the current mechanism is that open-term `normalize` returns `Unproven` ⇒ no
> request is created, and the representation layer’s `GenericArg::Const(ConstExpr)` can now hold
> `k-1`. **The adjudication changed to two orthogonal decisions plus one termination subdecision**:
> D-a representation (A: open terms enter `deferred` and are normalized before enqueueing / B: no
> specialization); D-b evaluation point (mono only; C-1, the second field, and C-2, reconstruction
> from IR, both break the single representation/evaluator, so C-3 = A); **F4-T termination** (naive
> firing pushes the recursion chain into `max_depth` and triggers E3005; T-a: condition folding
> within the instance / T-b: bounded firing + fallback to the original at the boundary / T-c:
> termination layer or specification change). **Do A first, then adjudicate; before measurement, “A
> is already implementable” must not be treated as a conclusion.** **User (2026-11-16):
> D-a=A-closed, D-b=C-3, F4-T=T-b, bound=8; C3 implementation is a separate task, and the complete
> I4 form is not claimed.**

> **B.d’s prerequisite: decision-point protocol (not yet written)**: RFC-027 §12.1 requires that
> decision points first use unification; after unification fails, equality may be used, but all
> partial bindings produced by the unification attempt must first be rolled back. The implementation
> must answer: is the entry point an outer wrapper around `solver.unify` or a new `decide` layer;
> when may defeq be attempted (only when both sides are ground and have no unbound variables); what
> are the snapshot/restoration boundary and cost; and do unify and defeq share one budget or have
> separate budgets. **Responsibility boundary**: unify remains responsible for subtyping/Any/Never;
> defeq only determines equivalence and does not take over subtyping, and bidirectional subtyping
> must not be treated as equivalence (RFC-027 §12.1). **Provide the protocol and production call
> graph before writing code**; 6.9.3c’s wiring contract wasted two rounds by assuming `can_assign`
> was the decision point, while `AssignmentChecker` has zero production instances.

> **E acceptance and residuals (I8, cross-cutting)**: every slice must provide ① zero
> corpus/IR-snapshot drift or an item-by-item classification; ② `testsuite run fast` + affected
> integration tests; ③ idempotence of the error-code generator (if emission points/code tables
> change); ④ classification of behavior changes under D59 and red before green. **Wiring tasks
> additionally require**: production-call-graph evidence (which production entry point, how many
> paths, which path is reachable); using unit-test reachability to impersonate user reachability is
> prohibited; rollback and budget require measured numbers. **Residual disposition**: #537 (class D,
> silent dropping of proof-function parameter refinement; not fixed before adjudication, and
> 6.9.4/6.9.5 must not depend on its fix), #539 (import-surface q=0; a class-C phase boundary; if it
> remains open, 6.9.4 covers only the module), #540 (deeply nested stack overflow;
> **closed**—3.20.30 closed on 2026-11-14: orchestration-layer 64MB large-stack thread + parser
> depth gate MAX_PARSE_DEPTH=1024 + E0019, see the 3.20.30 row in 09), #541 (TailCall;
> **closed**—three synchronized changes on 2026-11-16: `call_form_is_rewritten` /
> `replace_calls_in_function` / `drop_erased_call_args` cover `Call`/`TailCall` through the single
> `rewritten_call_target`; init sections and function bodies share one site-rewriting
> implementation; red→green + three mutations), #542 (orphaned shared layer; **landed
> (2026-11-19)**: DCE pass + post-mono two-entry wiring + C2c temporary-evasion removal; red→green,
> 402/402, function-table attribution and deviations), #552 (multi-parameter predicate proof
> execution; a neighboring area of 6.9.5, separate case). **Ownership remains unadjudicated**: the
> four open-constraint tripwires (`Eq(N, n)`) in `rfc027_refined_transparency` do not belong to
> 6.9.4’s constant-equality bridge; whether they remain in 6.9.4 outOfScope or move to 6.9.1/E4018’s
> proof-function surface requires adjudication.

> **Non-claims / boundaries**: this item covers only record Σ (B: witness field), does not touch
> Π/`DepFn`, and does not claim the complete Π/Σ form; 6.9.4 covers only constant equalities and
> does not fulfill the open-constraint `Eq(N,n)` tripwire; the second value position of `f(y)(y)` is
> not in this item; witness erasure ≠ Runtime check erasure; if #537 is not fixed, 6.9.4/6.9.5 cover
> only shapes that do not depend on proof-function parameter refinement; if #539 is not fixed, 6.9.4
> covers only the module; #540 is closed (3.20.30); #542 has landed (2026-11-19); 2.1.4’s
> inner-isolation red was cleared on 2026-11-20 (the criterion was rewritten by slot identity in the
> 2.1.4 closure) ⇒ **all prerequisites for promoting `verify` to a gate are cleared**.

**Hard acceptance** (**revision of 2026-10-27, following the 2026-10-26 decision ②**):
`git diff --stat tests/yaoxiang tests/baselines src/std/` must be empty in **each round of
6.1–6.7**. **Direct-driver test files** under `tests/` (tests that directly drive changed code, such
as `tests/formatter_integration.rs`) may be modified when mechanically required by the change, but
each must be registered with **file / line count / reason**; **tests must not be deleted, and
assertions must not be weakened**. **Exception**: 6.8.2 is a **behavioral fix** batch (new
diagnostic codes + baseline updates), does not fall under this hard acceptance criterion, and is a
separate round with separate adjudication.

> **Revision relationship**: the original wording of 2026-10-26 decision ② was
> `git diff --stat tests/ src/std/`, applying a blanket zero-difference requirement to the entire
> `tests/` directory; the revised wording **only changes direct-driver test files from “zero
> difference” to “may be changed but must be registered with file / line count / reason”**, while
> the **zero-difference requirement for the corpus tier (`tests/yaoxiang`), baseline tier
> (`tests/baselines`), and standard library (`src/std/`) remains unchanged**. First application =
> 6.1.2: only `tests/formatter_integration.rs` changed, by **3 lines** (2 changes from `i64` to
> `Int64` in `assert_format_eq` + 1 comment line), with the reason = the changed spelling requires
> the expected type name in that assertion to be synchronized; no tests were deleted and no
> assertions were weakened. See “P6 6.1 Name Normalization Closure”.

### P7 SSA Transformation of the Intermediate Representation

Source: [04](04-ssa.md) §Implementation Points batches a–d, criterion C4, **strictly serial within
each batch**.

- [x] **7.0 Pure-move splitting** (6 Level 3 tasks)

> **7.0 design basis and boundaries**: the target layout comes from the directory tree and migration
> table in [01-routing](01-routing.md)—`middle/ir/ir_gen.rs` → `middle/lower/`,
> `sema/inference/expressions.rs` → `sema/infer/` (the call-resolution family `call/`). This batch
> **only performs pure moves**, with the acceptance criterion of “zero semantic diff other than
> paths” (manual review of every file) + C1 snapshot zero-diff. It does not conflict with RFC-039’s
> [RFC-039](../../rfc/accepted/039-compiler-architecture.md) alternative A, “only split files, do
> not change the paradigm”: the rejected alternative used moves **instead of** paradigm work,
> whereas this batch is a **prerequisite** for paradigm work—7c.1’s `lower/synth.rs` depends on the
> `lower/` directory being in place, and 7d.1’s method-level split of `generate_call_expr_ir` is
> performed on the smaller files after the move. The expressions side follows the decision (split
> only the call-resolution family; leave `statements.rs` in place); the overall `inference/` →
> `infer/` rename and merging of statements belong to the full migration and are not in this batch;
> they are deferred to a separate design round after P7. `synth.rs` is a new boundary rather than a
> move and belongs to 7c.1. Survey baseline (2026-11-12): `ir_gen.rs` has 9,804 lines and 148
> methods, with the body in a single `impl AstToIrGenerator` block (approximately 9,250 lines);
> `expressions.rs` has 6,825 lines and 93 methods, with the body in a single
> `impl ExpressionInferrer` block (approximately 6,280 lines); the largest methods are
> `infer_call_expr` at 1,671 lines and `generate_call_expr_ir` at 866 lines (the latter is the
> target of 7d.1).

- [x] **7a Cut multiple definitions**
- [x] **7b Switch to the SSA form** (5 Level 3 tasks)

> **Retention condition for A/I4 (rewritten 2026-11-13)**: 6.9.2-C2c’s “conservatively retain the
> original” was a temporary form while I4 was incomplete—the original still carried q=0 slots, while
> the clone materialized in-body reads as constants. This evasion was removed when #542 landed
> (2026-11-19): mono no longer deletes originals by generic name, the restoration gate was removed,
> and deletion responsibility moved to post-mono DCE; the remaining “do not erase the original” is
> the long-term semantics of C3 T-b’s boundary fallback, not an evasion. **C3’s completion
> reassessment (2026-11-16)**: C3’s T-b boundary fallback leaves the ninth firing’s call site on the
> original ⇒ the original is put back and the shared layer still has referrers, so #542’s surface is
> narrowed to **non-recursive full-coverage shapes** (`fn_const_generic` family); disposition landed
> on 2026-11-19 (post-mono DCE wiring, together with removal of this evasion).

- [x] **7c Make implicit contracts explicit** ✅ 535933ce、4185dcdf、21ddf23f、138f9e0f

> **7c residuals (not from this commit, not fixed)**: ① calling a cross-unit single-definition
> method with named arguments reports E6007—identical in the old and new revisions (not introduced
> by this commit and not amplified by it; reverified on 2026-11-25). ② The refinement-predicate
> solver budget is fixed at 100ms (`src/frontend/core/sema/proof/budget.rs:41`, `:50`, passed to Z3
> through `src/frontend/core/sema/layers/predicate.rs:203`, `:239`): when solving exceeds this
> budget, execution falls into the Unknown/Unproven branch, and corpus entry
> `tests/yaoxiang/06-compile-errors/refinement/call_arg_no_evidence_err.yx` degrades from E4018 to
> E2031; measurement in the standard batch environment produced E4018 in 5/5 cases, while high load
> as a trigger condition was not reproduced. ③ 4.5.2’s persistent difference-set criterion for
> `overload_resolutions` (including the `examined`/`consumed` lower bounds on the evidence surface,
> `src/middle/ir/tests/overload_resolutions.rs`) no longer exists—**the wording was corrected on
> 2025-11-25**: the module header now states the facts (the original text was “the difference set
> must be empty”; it now records that this corpus-level case was removed during the batch retirement
> on 2026-11-07, that there is no automatic execution point in the repository, and that equivalent
> evidence is provided by `release_plan_spans`), and restoring the criterion must pass the three
> admission gates for tests.

**Risk**: the precedence table is the sole authority, and missing a consumer will **silently change
associativity** (no error, only a different parse result). The defenses are the permanent D1–D3
assertions and the operator-property cross-validation tests; every new operator must enter both the
table and the cross-validation tests.

> **8.3–8.6 depend on D0/D2 from `08`**: if a grammar file is placed outside `parser/`, or action
> code refers back to `sema`, it will be blocked by `check-boundary.py`.

### P9 Anti-Rebound Gates (Unified Script Inventory — Original Gap G3 Supplemented by This Document)

Source: [01](01-routing.md) §Anti-Rebound Mechanism + [08](08-maintenance-mechanism.md)
§Machine-Checkable Rules

**This is the sole authoritative inventory of all CI scripts** (10 active scripts; row 9.8 is
retained as a cancellation record). Scripts are connected to CI at their “introduction phase”; those
marked report-only are converted to hard failures uniformly in P9 (changing from “report” to
“fail”)—existing violations (parallel representations, disambiguation aliases, etc.) cannot reach
zero until P6/P8, and converting them to hard failures early would leave CI permanently red and
cause the gates to be manually bypassed. When P9 converts them to hard failures, the gates should
therefore be exactly green. P2’s 2.5, P4’s 4.3.2, and P7’s 7c.1 all refer to this table and do not
establish separate inventories.

      hard failures uniformly in P9** (row 9.8 was cancelled by the user on 2026-11-24; details are
      in the table below)
